s_{i}^{(0)}\leftarrow\mathrm{sign}(v_{i}^{(0)}\widehat{z}_{i}c_{l_{1}})

17:

w_{i}^{(0)}\leftarrow s_{i}^{(0)}(|\widehat{z}_{i}/(c_{l_{1}}(\alpha^{\top}V\widehat{u}_{i})^{{l_{1}-1}})|)^{1/(p+1)}V\widehat{u}_{i}

18:

return

v_{i}^{(0)}
,
w_{i}^{(0)}

19:

end

procedure

Sample complexity:
We use matrix Bernstein inequality to bound the error between
\widehat{P}_{2}
and
P_{2}
, which requires
\widetilde{\Omega}(d)
samples (Lemma
E.5
). To bound the estimation error between
R_{3}
and
\widehat{R}_{3}
, we flatten the tensor to a matrix and then use matrix Bernstein inequality to bound the error, which requires
\widetilde{\Omega}(k^{3})
samples (Lemma
E.10
). In Algorithm
4
, we also need to approximate a
\R^{d}
vector and a
\R^{k\times k}
matrix, which also requires
\widetilde{\Omega}(d)
. Thus, taking
\widetilde{O}(d)+\widetilde{O}(k^{3})
samples is sufficient.

Time complexity:
In Part a), by using a specially designed power method, we only need
O(knd)
time to compute the subspace estimation
V
. Part b) needs
O(knd)
to form
\widehat{R}_{3}
and the tensor factorization needs
O(k^{3})
time. Part c) requires calculation of
d\times k
and
k^{2}\times k
linear systems in Eq. (
28
), which takes at most
O(knd)
running time. Hence, the total time complexity is
O(knd)
.

E.2
Main Result for Tensor Methods

The goal of this Section is to prove Theorem
5.6
.

Theorem
5.6
.

Let the activation function be homogeneous satisfying Assumption
5.3
. For any
0<\epsilon<1
and
t\geq 1
, if
|S|\geq\epsilon^{-2}\cdot d\cdot\poly(t,k,\kappa,\log d)
, then there exists an algorithm (Algorithm
1
) that takes
|S|k\cdot\widetilde{O}(d)
time and outputs a matrix
W^{(0)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
and a vector
v^{(0)}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
such that, with probability at least
1-d^{-\Omega(t)}
,

\displaystyle\|W^{(0)}-W^{*}\|_{F}\leq\epsilon\cdot\poly(k,\kappa)\|W^{*}\|_{F},\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }v_{i}^{(0)}=v_{i}^{*}.

Proof.

The success of Algorithm
1
depends on two approximations. The first is the estimation of the normalized
w_{i}^{*}
up to some unknown sign flip, i.e., the error
\|\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-s_{i}V\widehat{u}_{i}\|
for some
s_{i}\in\{-1,1\}
. The second is the estimation of the magnitude of
w_{i}^{*}
and the signs
v_{i}^{*},s_{i}
which is conducted in Algorithm
4
.

For the first one,

\displaystyle\|\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-s_{i}V\widehat{u}_{i}\|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|+\|VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-Vs_{i}\widehat{u}_{i}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|+\|V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-s_{i}\widehat{u}_{i}\|,

(29)

where the first step follows by triangle inequality, the second step follows by
V^{\top}V=I
.

We can upper bound
\|VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|
,

\displaystyle\|VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2})+\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\poly(k,\kappa)\|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|+\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \poly(k,\kappa)\epsilon,

(30)

where the first step follows by Lemma
E.6
, the second step follows by
\sigma_{k}(P_{2})\geq 1/\poly(k,\kappa)
, and the last step follows by
\|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|\leq\epsilon\poly(k,\kappa)
if the number of samples is proportional to
\widetilde{O}(d/\epsilon^{2})
as shown in Lemma
E.5
.

We can upper bound
\|V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-s_{i}\widehat{u}_{i}\|
,

\displaystyle\|V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-s_{i}\widehat{u}_{i}\|\leq\poly(k,\kappa)\|\widehat{R}_{3}-R_{3}\|\leq\epsilon\poly(k,\kappa),

(31)

where the first step follows by Theorem 3 in
[
KCL15
]
, and the last step follows by
\|\widehat{R}_{3}-R_{3}\|\leq\epsilon\poly(k,\kappa)
if the number of samples is proportional to
\widetilde{O}(k^{2}/\epsilon^{2})
as shown in Lemma
E.10
.

Combining Eq. (
E.2
), (
E.2
) and (
31
) together,

\displaystyle\|\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-s_{i}V\widehat{u}_{i}\|\leq\epsilon\poly(k,\kappa).

For the second one, we can bound the error of the estimation of moments,
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
, using number of samples proportional to
\widetilde{O}(d)
by Lemma
E.12
and Lemma
E.5
respectively. The error of the solutions of the linear systems Eq.(
28
) can be bounded by
\|Q_{1}-\widehat{Q}_{1}\|,\|Q_{2}-\widehat{Q}_{2}\|,\|\widehat{u}_{i}-V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|
and
\|(I-VV^{\top})\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|
according to Lemma
E.13
and Lemma
E.14
. Then we can bound the error of the output of Algorithm
4
. Furthermore, since
v_{i}^{*}
’s are discrete values, they can be exactly recovered. All the sample complexities mentioned in the above lemmata are linear in dimension and polynomial in other factors to achieve a constant error. So accumulating all these errors we complete our proof.

∎

Remark E.1
.

The proofs of these lemmata for Theorem
1
can be found in the following sections. Note that these lemmata also hold for any activations satisfying
Property
3.1
and Assumption
5.3
.
However, since we are unclear how to implement the last step of
Algorithm
1
(Algorithm
4
)
for general non-homogeneous activations, we restrict our theorem to homogeneous activations only.

E.3
Error Bound for the Subspace Spanned by the Weight Matrix

E.3.1
Error Bound for the Second-order Moment in Different Cases

Lemma E.2
.

Let
M_{2}
be defined as in Definition
5.1
. Let
\widehat{M}_{2}
be the empirical version of
M_{2}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{2}=\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{(x,y)\in S}y\cdot(x\otimes x-I_{d}),

where
S
denote a set of samples from Distribution
\D
defined in Eq. (
1
).
Assume
M_{2}\neq 0
, i.e.,
m_{i}^{(2)}\neq 0
for any
i
.
Then for any
0<\epsilon<1,t\geq 1
, if

\displaystyle|S|\geq\max_{i\in[k]}(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}/|m_{2,i}|+1)\cdot\epsilon^{-2}d\poly(\log d,t)

with probability at least
1-d^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|M_{2}-\widehat{M}_{2}\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{2,i}|.

Proof.

Recall that, for each sample
(x,y)
,
y=\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}^{*}\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)
. We consider each component
i\in[k]
.
Define function
B_{i}(x):\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that

\displaystyle B_{i}(x)=\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot(x\otimes x-I_{d}).

Define
g(z)=\phi(z)-\phi(0)
, then
|g(z)|=|\int_{0}^{z}\phi^{\prime}(s)\mathrm{d}s|\leq L_{1}/(p+1)|z|^{p+1}
, which follows Property
3.1
.

(I) Bounding
\|B_{i}(x)\|
.

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\frac{L_{1}}{p+1}|w_{i}^{*\top}x|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)(\|x_{j}\|^{2}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\frac{L_{1}}{p+1}\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)d\poly(\log d,t)

where the last step follows by Fact
B.1
and Fact
B.2
.

(II) Bounding
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|
.

Note that
\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]=m_{2,i}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}
. Therefore,
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|=|m_{2,i}|
.

(III) Bounding
\max(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\|B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}\|,\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\|B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)\|)
.

Note that
B_{i}(x)
is a symmetric matrix, thus it suffices to only bound one of them.

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}^{2}(x)]\right\|\lesssim\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\|x\|^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\lesssim(\frac{L_{1}}{p+1}\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}d.

(IV) Bounding
\max_{\|a\|=\|b\|=1}(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)b)^{2}])
.

Note that
B_{i}(x)
is a symmetric matrix, thus it suffices to consider the case where
a=b
.

\displaystyle\max_{\|a\|=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)a)^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\phi^{4}(w_{i}^{*\top}x)]\right)^{1/4}\lesssim\frac{L_{1}}{p+1}\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|.

Define
L=\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|
.
Then we have for any
0<\epsilon<1
, if

n\gtrsim\frac{L^{2}d+|m_{2,i}|^{2}+L|m_{2,i}|d\cdot\poly(\log d,t)\epsilon}{\epsilon^{2}|m_{2,i}|^{2}}t\log d

with probability at least
1-1/d^{t}
,

\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]-\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{x\in S}B_{i}(x)\right\|\leq\epsilon|m_{2,i}|.

∎

Lemma E.3
.

Let
M_{3}
be defined as in Definition
5.1
. Let
\widehat{M}_{3}
be the empirical version of
M_{3}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{3}=\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{(x,y)\in S}y\cdot(x^{\otimes 3}-x\widetilde{\otimes}I),

where
S
denote a set of samples (each sample is i.i.d. sampled from Distribution
\D
defined in Eq. (
1
)).
Assume
M_{3}\neq 0
, i.e.,
m_{3,i}\neq 0
for any
i
. Let
\alpha
be a fixed unit vector.
Then for any
0<\epsilon<1,t\geq 1
, if

|S|\geq\max_{i\in[k]}(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}/|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)|^{2}+1)\cdot\epsilon^{-2}d\poly(\log d,t)

with probability at least
1-1/d^{t}
,

\displaystyle\|M_{3}(I,I,\alpha)-\widehat{M}_{3}(I,I,\alpha)\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)|.

Proof.

Since
y=\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}^{*}\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)
. We consider each component
i\in[k]
.

Define function
B_{i}(x):\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that

\displaystyle B_{i}(x)=[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot(x^{\otimes 3}-x\widetilde{\otimes}I)](I,I,\alpha)=\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot((x^{\top}\alpha)x^{\otimes 2}-\alpha^{\top}xI-\alpha x^{\top}-x\alpha^{\top}).

Define
g(z)=\phi(z)-\phi(0)
, then
|g(z)|=|\int_{0}^{z}\phi^{\prime}(s)ds|\leq\frac{L_{1}}{p+1}|z|^{p+1}\lesssim|z|^{p+1}
, which follows Property
3.1
. In order to apply Lemma
B.7
, we need to bound the following four quantities,

(I) Bounding
\|B_{i}(x)\|
.

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot((x^{\top}\alpha)x^{\otimes 2}-\alpha^{\top}xI_{d}-\alpha x^{\top}-x\alpha^{\top})\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)|\cdot\|(x^{\top}\alpha)x^{\otimes 2}-\alpha^{\top}xI-\alpha x^{\top}-x\alpha^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (|w_{i}^{*\top}x|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)\|(x^{\top}\alpha)x^{\otimes 2}-\alpha^{\top}xI-\alpha x^{\top}-x\alpha^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (|w_{i}^{*\top}x|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)(|x^{\top}\alpha|\|x\|^{2}+3|\alpha^{\top}x|),

where the third step follows by definition of
g(z)
, and last step follows by definition of spectral norm and triangle inequality.

Using Fact
B.1
and Fact
B.2
, we have for any constant
t\geq 1
, with probability
1-1/(nd^{4t})
,

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\lesssim(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)d\poly(\log d,t).

(II) Bounding
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|
.

Note that
\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]=m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}
. Therefore,
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|=|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)|
.

(III) Bounding
\max(\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}]\|,\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)]\|)
.

Because matrix
B_{i}(x)
is symmetric, thus it suffices to bound one of them,

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}^{2}(x)]\right\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(x^{\top}\alpha)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\|x\|^{4}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}d.

(IV) Bounding
\max_{\|a\|=\|b\|=1}(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)b)^{2}])^{1/2}
.

\displaystyle\max_{\|a\|=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)a)^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\phi^{4}(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\right]\right)^{1/4}\lesssim\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|.

Define
L=\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|
. Then we have for any
0<\epsilon<1
, if

|S|\gtrsim\frac{L^{2}d+|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)|^{2}+L|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)|d\cdot\poly(\log d,t)\epsilon}{\epsilon^{2}|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)|^{2}}\cdot t\log d

with probability at least
1-d^{-t}
,

\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]-\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{x\in S}B_{i}(x)\right\|\leq\epsilon|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)|.

∎

Lemma E.4
.

Let
M_{4}
be defined as in Definition
5.1
. Let
\widehat{M}_{4}
be the empirical version of
M_{4}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{4}=\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{(x,y)\in S}y\cdot(x^{\otimes 4}-(x\otimes x)\widetilde{\otimes}I+I\widetilde{\otimes}I),

where
S
denote a set of samples (where each sample is i.i.d. sampled are sampled from Distribution
\D
defined in Eq. (
1
)).
Assume
M_{4}\neq 0
, i.e.,
m_{4,i}\neq 0
for any
i
. Let
\alpha
be a fixed unit vector.
Then for any
0<\epsilon<1,t\geq 1
, if

|S|\geq\max_{i\in[k]}(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}/|m_{4,i}|(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}+1)^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{-2}\cdot d\poly(\log d,t)

with probability at least
1-1/d^{t}
,

\|M_{4}(I,I,\alpha,\alpha)-\widehat{M}_{4}(I,I,\alpha,\alpha)\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{4,i}|(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}.

Proof.

Since
y=\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}^{*}\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)
. We consider each component
i\in[k]
.

Define function
B_{i}(x):\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that

\displaystyle B_{i}(x)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ [\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot(x^{\otimes 4}-(x\otimes x)\widetilde{\otimes}I+I\widetilde{\otimes}I)](I,I,\alpha,\alpha)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot((x^{\top}\alpha)^{2}x^{\otimes 2}-(\alpha^{\top}x)^{2}I-2(\alpha^{\top}x)(x\alpha^{\top}+\alpha x^{\top})-xx^{\top}+2\alpha\alpha^{\top}+I).

Define
g(z)=\phi(z)-\phi(0)
, then
|g(z)|=|\int_{0}^{z}\phi^{\prime}(s)ds|\leq L_{1}/(p+1)|z|^{p+1}\lesssim|z|^{p+1}
, which follows Property
3.1
.

(I) Bounding
\|B_{i}(x)\|
.

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\lesssim

\displaystyle|\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)|\cdot((x^{\top}\alpha)^{2}\|x\|^{2}+1+\|x\|^{2}+(\alpha^{\top}x)^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(|w_{i}^{*\top}x|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)\cdot((x^{\top}\alpha)^{2}\|x\|^{2}+1+\|x\|^{2}+(\alpha^{\top}x)^{2})

Using Fact
B.1
and Fact
B.2
, we have for any constant
t\geq 1
, with probability
1-1/(nd^{4t})
,

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\lesssim(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)d\poly(\log d,t).

(II) Bounding
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|
.

Note that
\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]=m_{4,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}
. Therefore,
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|=|m_{4,i}|(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}
.

(III) Bounding
\max(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\|B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}\|,\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\|B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)\|)
.

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)^{2}]\right\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(x^{\top}\alpha)^{8}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\|x\|^{4}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}d.

(IV) Bounding
\max_{\|a\|=\|b\|=1}(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)b)^{2}])^{1/2}
.

\displaystyle\max_{\|a\|=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)a)^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\phi^{4}(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\right]\right)^{1/4}\lesssim\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|.

Define
L=\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|
.
Then we have for any
0<\epsilon<1
, if

n\gtrsim\frac{L^{2}d+|m_{4,i}|^{2}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{4}+L|m_{4,i}|(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}d\poly(\log d,t)\epsilon}{\epsilon^{2}|m_{4,i}|^{2}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{4}}\cdot t\log d

with probability at least
1-d^{-t}
,

\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]-\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{x\in S}B_{i}(x)\right\|\leq\epsilon|m_{4,i}|(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}.

∎

E.3.2
Error Bound for the Second-order Moment

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
E.5
, which shows we can approximate the second order moments up to some precision by using linear sample complexity in
d
.

Lemma E.5

(Estimation of the second order moment)
.

Let
P_{2}
and
j_{2}
be defined in Definition
5.4
.
Let
S
denote a set of i.i.d. samples generated from distribution
{\cal D}
(defined in (
1
)). Let
\widehat{P}_{2}
be the empirical version of
P_{2}
using dataset
S
, i.e.,
\widehat{P}_{2}=\E_{S}[P_{2}]
. Assume the activation function satisfies Property
3.1
and Assumption
5.3
. Then for any
0<\epsilon<1
and
t\geq 1
, and
m_{0}=\min_{i\in[k]}\{|m_{j_{2},i}|^{2}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2(j_{2}-2)}\}
, if

|S|\gtrsim\sigma_{1}^{2p+2}\cdot d\cdot\poly(t,\log d)/(\epsilon^{2}m_{0}),

then with probability at least
1-d^{-\Omega(t)}
,

\displaystyle\|P_{2}-\widehat{P}_{2}\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{j_{2},i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{j_{2}-2}|.

Proof.

We have shown the bound for
j_{2}=2,3,4
in Lemma
E.2
, Lemma
E.3
and Lemma
E.4
respectively.
To summarize, for any
0<\epsilon<1
we have if

|S|\geq\max_{i\in[k]}\left\{\frac{(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|+|m_{j_{2},i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{(j_{2}-2)}|)^{2}}{|m_{j_{2},i}|^{2}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2(j_{2}-2)}}\right\}\cdot\epsilon^{-2}d\poly(\log d,t)

with probability at least
1-d^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|P_{2}-\widehat{P}_{2}\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{j_{2},i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{j_{2}-2}|.

∎

E.3.3
Subspace Estimation Using Power Method

Lemma
E.6
shows a small number of power iterations can estimate the subspace of
\{w_{i}^{*}\}_{i\in[k]}
to some precision, which provides guarantees for Algorithm
3
.

Lemma E.6

(Bound on subspace estimation)
.

Let
P_{2}
be defined as in Definition.
5.4
and
\widehat{P}_{2}
be its empirical version. Let
U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
be the orthogonal column span of
W^{*}\in\R^{d\times k}
. Assume
\|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|\leq\sigma_{k}(P_{2})/10
. Let
C
be a large enough positive number such that
C>2\|P_{2}\|
.
Then after
T=O(\log(1/\epsilon))
iterations, the output of Algorithm
3
,
V\in\R^{d\times k}
, will satisfy

\displaystyle\|UU^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|\lesssim\|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2})+\epsilon,

which implies

\displaystyle\|(I-VV^{\top})w_{i}^{*}\|\lesssim(\|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2})+\epsilon)\|w_{i}^{*}\|.

Proof.

According to Weyl’s inequality, we are able to pick up the correct numbers of positive eigenvalues and negative eigenvalues in Algorithm
3
as long as
\widehat{P}_{2}
and
P_{2}
are close enough.

Let
U=[U_{1}\;U_{2}]\in\R^{d\times k}
be the eigenspace of
\text{span}\{w_{1}^{*}\;w_{2}^{*}\;\cdots\;w_{k}^{*}\}
, where
U_{1}\in\R^{d\times k_{1}}
corresponds to positive eigenvalues of
P_{2}
and
U_{2}\in\R^{d\times k_{2}}
is for negatives.

Let
\overline{V}_{1}
be the top-
k_{1}
eigenvectors of
CI+\widehat{P}_{2}
. Let
\overline{V}_{2}
be the top-
k_{2}
eigenvectors of
CI-\widehat{P}_{2}
. Let
\overline{V}=[\overline{V}_{1}\;\overline{V}_{2}]\in\R^{d\times k}
.

According to Lemma 9 in
[
HK13
]
, we have

\|(I-U_{1}U_{1}^{\top})\overline{V}_{1}\|\lesssim\|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2})
,
\|(I-U_{2}U_{2}^{\top})\overline{V}_{2}\|\lesssim\|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2})
. Using Fact
B.4
, we have
\|(I-UU^{\top})\overline{V}\|=\|UU^{\top}-\overline{V}\overline{V}^{\top}\|
.

Let
\epsilon
be the precision we want to achieve using power method. Let
V_{1}
be the top-
k_{1}
eigenvectors returned after
O(\log(1/\epsilon))
iterations of power methods on
CI+\widehat{P}_{2}
and
V_{2}\in\R^{d\times k_{2}}
for
CI-\widehat{P}_{2}
similarly.

According to Theorem 7.2 in
[
AKZ12
]
, we have
\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|\leq\epsilon
and
\|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|\leq\epsilon
.

Let
U_{\perp}
be the complementary matrix of
U
. Then we have,

\displaystyle\|(I-U_{1}U_{1}^{\top})\overline{V}_{1}\|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max_{\|a\|=1}\|(I-U_{1}U_{1}^{\top})\overline{V}_{1}a\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max_{\|a\|=1}\|(U_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}+U_{2}U_{2}^{\top})\overline{V}_{1}a\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max_{\|a\|=1}\sqrt{\|U_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}\overline{V}_{1}a\|^{2}+\|U_{2}U_{2}^{\top}\overline{V}_{1}a\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max_{\|a\|=1}\|U_{2}U_{2}^{\top}\overline{V}_{1}a\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|U_{2}U_{2}^{\top}\overline{V}_{1}\|,

(32)

where the first step follows by definition of spectral norm, the second step follows by
I=U_{1}U_{1}^{\top}+U_{2}U_{2}^{\top}+U_{\perp}^{\top}U_{\perp}^{\top}
, the third step follows by
U_{2}^{\top}U_{\perp}=0
, and last step follows by definition of spectral norm.

We can upper bound
\|(I-UU^{\top})\overline{V}\|
,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(I-UU^{\top})\overline{V}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|(I-U_{1}U_{1}^{\top})\overline{V}_{1}\|+\|(I-U_{2}U_{2}^{\top})\overline{V}_{2}\|+\|U_{2}U_{2}^{\top}\overline{V}_{1}\|+\|U_{1}U_{1}^{\top}\overline{V}_{2}\|)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ 2(\|(I-U_{1}U_{1}^{\top})\overline{V}_{1}\|+\|(I-U_{2}U_{2}^{\top})\overline{V}_{2}\|)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2}),

(33)

where the first step follows by triangle inequality, the second step follows by Eq. (
E.3.3
), and the last step follows by Lemma 9 in
[
HK13
]
.

We define matrix
R
such that
\overline{V}_{2}R=(I-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top})V_{2}
is the QR decomposition of
(I-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top})V_{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})\overline{V}_{2}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})(I-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top})V_{2}R^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})(I-\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}+\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top})V_{2}R^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underbrace{\|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})(I-\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top})V_{2}R^{-1}\|}_{\alpha}+\underbrace{\|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})\|\|R^{-1}\|\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|}_{\beta},

where the first step follows by
\overline{V}_{2}=(I-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top})V_{2}R^{-1}
, and the last step follows by triangle inequality.

Furthermore, we have,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \alpha+\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top})V_{2}R^{-1}\|+\|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})\|\|R^{-1}\|\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})V_{2}R^{-1}\|+\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}V_{2}R^{-1}\|+\|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})\|\|R^{-1}\|\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|\|R^{-1}\|+\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}V_{2}\|\|R^{-1}\|+\|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})\|\|R^{-1}\|\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|\|R^{-1}\|+\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}V_{2}\|\|R^{-1}\|+\|R^{-1}\|\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|\|R^{-1}\|+\|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})V_{2}\|\|R^{-1}\|+\|R^{-1}\|\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (2\|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|+\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|)\|R^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ 3\epsilon\|R^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ 6\epsilon,

where the first step follows by definition of
\alpha,\beta
, the second step follows by triangle inequality, the third step follows by
\|AB\|\leq\|A\|\|B\|
, the fourth step follows by
\|(I-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})\|=1
, the fifth step follows by Eq. (
E.3.3
), the sixth step follows by Fact
B.4
, the seventh step follows by
\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|\leq\epsilon
and
\|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|\leq\epsilon
, and the last step follows by
\|R^{-1}\|\leq 2
(Claim
E.7
).

Finally,

\displaystyle\|UU^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|UU^{\top}-\overline{V}\overline{V}^{\top}\|+\|\overline{V}\overline{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(I-UU^{\top})\overline{V}\|+\|\overline{V}\overline{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2})+\|\overline{V}\overline{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2})+\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|+\|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{P}_{2}-P_{2}\|/\sigma_{k}(P_{2})+2\epsilon,

where the first step follows by triangle inequality, the second step follows by Fact
B.4
, the third step follows by Eq. (
E.3.3
), the fourth step follows by triangle inequality, and the last step follows by
\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|\leq\epsilon
and
\|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|\leq\epsilon
.

Therefore we finish the proof.
∎

It remains to prove Claim
E.7
.

Claim E.7
.

\sigma_{k}(R)\geq 1/2
.

Proof.

First, we can rewrite
R^{\top}R
in the follow way,

\displaystyle R^{\top}R=V_{2}^{\top}(I-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top})V_{2}=I-V_{2}^{\top}V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}V_{2}

Second, we can upper bound
\|V_{2}^{\top}V_{1}\|
by
1/4
,

\displaystyle\|V_{2}^{\top}V_{1}\|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}V_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})V_{1}\|+\|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}V_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})V_{1}\|+\|\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}(V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}-\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top})\|+\|\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top})V_{1}\|+\|\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}(V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}-\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top})\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}-\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}\|\cdot\|V_{1}\|+\|\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}\|\cdot\|V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}-\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \epsilon+\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ 1/4,

where the first step follows by
V_{2}^{\top}V_{2}=I
, the second step follows by triangle inequality, the third step follows by triangle inequality, the fourth step follows by
\|\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}\|=0
, the fifth step follows by
\|AB\|\leq\|A\|\cdot\|B\|
, and the last step follows by
\|\overline{V}_{1}\overline{V}_{1}^{\top}-V_{1}V_{1}^{\top}\|\leq\epsilon
,
\|V_{1}\|=1
,
\|\overline{V}_{2}\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}-V_{2}V_{2}^{\top}\|\leq\epsilon
and
\|\overline{V}_{2}^{\top}\|=1
, and the last step follows by
\epsilon<1/8
.

Thus, we can lower bound
\sigma_{k}^{2}(R)
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}^{2}(R)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lambda_{\min}(R^{\top}R)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{\|a\|=1}a^{\top}R^{\top}Ra

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{\|a\|=1}a^{\top}Ia-\|V_{2}^{\top}V_{1}a\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ 1-\max_{\|a\|=1}\|V_{2}^{\top}V_{1}a\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ 1-\|V_{2}^{\top}V_{1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ 3/4

which implies
\sigma_{k}(R)\geq 1/2
.
∎

E.4
Error Bound for the Reduced Third-order Moment

E.4.1
Error Bound for the Reduced Third-order Moment in Different Cases

Lemma E.8
.

Let
M_{3}
be defined as in Definition
5.1
. Let
\widehat{M}_{3}
be the empirical version of
M_{3}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{3}=\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{(x,y)\in S}y\cdot(x^{\otimes 3}-x\widetilde{\otimes}I),

where
S
denote a set of samples (where each sample is i.i.d. sampled from Distribution
\D
defined in Eq. (
1
)).
Assume
M_{3}\neq 0
, i.e.,
m_{3,i}\neq 0
for any
i
. Let
V\in\R^{d\times k}
be an orthogonal matrix satisfying
\|UU^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|\leq 1/4
, where
U
is the orthogonal basis of
\mathrm{span}\{w_{1}^{*},w_{2}^{*},\cdots,w_{k}^{*}\}
.
Then for any
0<\epsilon<1,t\geq 1
, if

|S|\geq\max_{i\in[k]}(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}/|m_{3,i}|^{2}+1)\cdot\epsilon^{-2}\cdot k^{2}\poly(\log d,t)

with probability at least
1-1/d^{t}
,

\displaystyle\|M_{3}(V,V,V)-\widehat{M}_{3}(V,V,V)\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{3,i}|.

Proof.

Since
y=\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}^{*}\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)
. We consider each component
i\in[k]
. We define function
T_{i}(x):\R^{d}\rightarrow\R^{k\times k\times k}
such that,

\displaystyle T_{i}(x)=\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot((V^{\top}x)^{\otimes 3}-(V^{\top}x)\widetilde{\otimes}I).

We flatten tensor
T_{i}(x)
along the first dimension into matrix
B_{i}(x)\in\R^{k\times k^{2}}
. Define
g(z)=\phi(z)-\phi(0)
, then
|g(z)|=|\int_{0}^{z}\phi^{\prime}(s)ds|\leq L_{1}/(p+1)|z|^{p+1}
, which follows Property
3.1
.

(I) Bounding
\|B_{i}(x)\|
.

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)|\cdot(\|V^{\top}x\|^{3}+3k\|V^{\top}x\|)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (|w_{i}^{*\top}x|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)\cdot(\|V^{\top}x\|^{3}+3k\|V^{\top}x_{j}\|)

Note that
V^{\top}x\sim\N(0,I_{k})
. According to Fact
B.1
and Fact
B.2
, we have for any constant
t\geq 1
, with probability
1-1/(nd^{t})
,

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\lesssim(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)k^{3/2}\poly(\log d,t)

(II) Bounding
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|
.

Note that
\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]=m_{3,i}(V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})\text{vec}((V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})(V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})^{\top})^{\top}
. Therefore,
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|=|m_{3,i}|\|V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|^{3}
. Since
\|VV^{\top}-UU^{\top}\|\leq 1/4
,
\|VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\leq 1/4
and
3/4\leq\|V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\leq 5/4
. So
\frac{1}{4}|m_{3,i}|\leq\|B\|\leq 2|m_{3,i}|
.

(III) Bounding
\max(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\|B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}\|,\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\|B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)\|)
.

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}]\right\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\|V^{\top}x\|^{6}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}k^{3/2}.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)]\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\|V^{\top}x\|^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\max_{\|A\|_{F}=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\langle A,(V^{\top}x)(V^{\top}x)^{\top}\rangle^{4}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}k^{2}.

(IV) Bounding
\max_{\|a\|=\|b\|=1}(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)b)^{2}])
.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max_{\|a\|=\|b\|=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)b)^{2}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x))^{4}]\right)^{1/4}\max_{\|a\|=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(a^{\top}V^{\top}x)^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\max_{\|A\|_{F}=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\langle A,(V^{\top}x)(V^{\top}x)^{\top}\rangle^{4}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)k

Define
L=\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|
.
Then we have for any
0<\epsilon<1
, if

|S|\gtrsim\frac{L^{2}k^{2}+|m_{3,i}|^{2}+k^{3/2}\poly(\log d,t)|m_{3,i}|\epsilon}{\epsilon^{2}|m_{3,i}|^{2}}t\log(k)

with probability at least
1-k^{-t}
,

\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]-\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{x\in S}B_{i}(x)\right\|\leq\epsilon|m_{3,i}|.

We can set
t=T\log(d)/\log(k)
, then if

|S|\geq\epsilon^{-2}(1+1/|m_{3,i}|^{2})\poly(T,\log d)

with probability at least
1-d^{-T}
,

\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]-\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{x\in S}B_{i}(x)\right\|\leq\epsilon|m_{3,i}|.

Also note that for any symmetric 3rd-order tensor
R
, the operator norm of
R
,

\displaystyle\|R\|=\max_{\|a\|=1}|R(a,a,a)|\leq\max_{\|a\|=1}\|R(a,I,I)\|_{F}=\|R^{(1)}\|.

∎

Lemma E.9
.

Let
M_{4}
be defined as in Definition
5.1
. Let
\widehat{M}_{4}
be the empirical version of
M_{4}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{4}=\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{(x,y)\in S}y\cdot\left(x^{\otimes 4}-(x\otimes x)\widetilde{\otimes}I+I\widetilde{\otimes}I\right),

where
S
is a set of samples (where each sample is i.i.d. sampled from Distribution
\D
defined in Eq. (
1
)).
Assume
M_{4}\neq 0
, i.e.,
m_{4,i}\neq 0
for any
i
. Let
\alpha
be a fixed unit vector. Let
V\in\R^{d\times k}
be an orthogonal matrix satisfying
\|UU^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|\leq 1/4
, where
U
is the orthogonal basis of
\text{span}\{w_{1}^{*},w_{2}^{*},\cdots,w_{k}^{*}\}
.
Then for any
0<\epsilon<1,t\geq 1
, if

\displaystyle|S|\geq\max_{i\in[k]}(1+\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}/|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|^{2})\cdot\epsilon^{-2}\cdot k^{2}\poly(\log d,t)

with probability at least
1-d^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|M_{4}(V,V,V,\alpha)-\widehat{M}_{4}(V,V,V,\alpha)\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|.

Proof.

Recall that for each
(x,y)\in S
, we have
y=\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}^{*}\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)
. We consider each component
i\in[k]
.
We define function
r(x):\R^{d}\rightarrow\R^{k}
such that

\displaystyle r(x)=V^{\top}x.

Define function
T_{i}(x):\R^{d}\rightarrow\R^{k\times k\times k}
such that

\displaystyle T_{i}(x)=\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\left(x^{\top}\alpha\cdot r(x)\otimes r(x)\otimes r(x)-(V^{\top}\alpha)\widetilde{\otimes}(r(x)\otimes r(x))-\alpha^{\top}x\cdot r(x)\widetilde{\otimes}I+(V^{\top}\alpha)\widetilde{\otimes}I\right).

We flatten
T_{i}(x):\R^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{k\times k\times k}
along the first dimension to obtain function
B_{i}(x):\R^{d}\rightarrow\R^{k\times k^{2}}
.
Define
g(z)=\phi(z)-\phi(0)
, then
|g(z)|=|\int_{0}^{z}\phi^{\prime}(s)ds|\leq L_{1}/(p+1)|z|^{p+1}
, which follows Property
3.1
.

(I) Bounding
\|B_{i}(x)\|
.

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\lesssim

\displaystyle(|w_{i}^{*\top}x|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)\cdot(|(x^{\top}\alpha)|\|V^{\top}x\|^{3}+3\|V^{\top}\alpha\|\|V^{\top}x\|^{2}

\displaystyle+3|(x^{\top}\alpha)|\|V^{\top}x_{j}\|\sqrt{k}+3\|V^{\top}\alpha\|\sqrt{k})

Note that
V^{\top}x\sim\N(0,I_{k})
. According to Fact
B.1
and Fact
B.2
, we have for any constant
t\geq 1
, with probability
1-1/(nd^{t})
,

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\lesssim(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)k^{3/2}\poly(\log d,t)

(II) Bounding
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|
.

Note that
\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]=m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})(V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})\text{vec}((V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})(V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})^{\top})^{\top}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\right\|=|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|\|V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|^{3}.

Since
\|VV^{\top}-UU^{\top}\|\leq 1/4
,
\|VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\leq 1/4
and
3/4\leq\|V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\leq 5/4
. So
\frac{1}{4}|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|\leq\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|\leq 2|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|
.

(III) Bounding
\max(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}],\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)])
.

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}]\right\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(\alpha^{\top}x)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\|V^{\top}x\|^{6}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}k^{3/2}.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)]\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(\alpha^{\top}x)^{4}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\|V^{\top}x\|^{4}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \cdot\left(\max_{\|A\|_{F}=1}\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\langle A,(V^{\top}x)(V^{\top}x)^{\top}\rangle^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}k^{2}.

(IV) Bounding
\max_{\|a\|=\|b\|=1}(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)b)^{2}\right])^{1/2}
.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max_{\|a\|=\|b\|=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)b)^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[\phi^{4}(w_{i}^{*\top}x)]\right)^{1/4}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(\alpha^{\top}x)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/4}\max_{\|a\|=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(a^{\top}V^{\top}x)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \cdot\max_{\|A\|_{F}=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\langle A,(V^{\top}x)(V^{\top}x)^{\top}\rangle^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)k.

Define
L=\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|
.
Then we have for any
0<\epsilon<1
, if

|S|\geq\frac{L^{2}k^{2}+|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|^{2}+k^{3/2}\poly(t,\log d)|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|\epsilon}{\epsilon^{2}(m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}))^{2}}\cdot t\log k

with probability at least
1-k^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]-\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{x\in S}^{n}B_{i}(x)\right\|\leq\epsilon|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|.

(34)

We can set
t=T\log(d)/\log(k)
, then if

|S|\geq\frac{(L+|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|)^{2}k^{2}\poly(T,\log d)}{\epsilon^{2}|m_{4,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})|^{2}}\cdot T\log^{2}d

with probability at least
1-d^{-T}
, Eq. (
34
) holds.
Also note that for any symmetric 3rd-order tensor
R
, the operator norm of
R
,

\displaystyle\|R\|=\max_{\|a\|=1}|R(a,a,a)|\leq\max_{\|a\|=1}\|R(a,I,I)\|_{F}=\|R^{(1)}\|.

∎

E.4.2
Final Error Bound for the Reduced Third-order Moment

Lemma
E.10
shows
\widehat{R}_{3}
can approximate
R_{3}
to some small precision with
\poly(k)
samples.

Lemma E.10

(Estimation of the reduced third order moment)
.

Let
U\in\R^{d\times k}
denote the orthogonal column span of
W^{*}
.
Let
\alpha
be a fixed unit vector and
V\in\R^{d\times k}
denote an orthogonal matrix satisfying
\|VV^{\top}-UU^{\top}\|\leq 1/4
. Define

R_{3}:=P_{3}(V,V,V)
, where
P_{3}
is defined as in Definition
5.4
using
\alpha
.
Let
\widehat{R}_{3}
be the empirical version of
R_{3}
using dataset
S
, where each sample of
S
is i.i.d. sampled from distribution
{\cal D}
(defined in (
1
)). Assume the activation function satisfies Property
3.1
and Assumption
5.3
. Then for any
0<\epsilon<1
and
t\geq 1
, define
j_{3}=\min\{j\geq 3|M_{j}\neq 0\}
and
m_{0}=\min_{i\in[k]}\{(m_{i}^{(j_{3})}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})^{j_{3}-3})^{2}\}
, if

|S|\geq\sigma_{1}^{2p+2}\cdot k^{2}\cdot\poly(\log d,t)/(\epsilon^{2}m_{0})

then we have ,

\displaystyle\|R_{3}-\widehat{R}_{3}\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{j_{3},i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{j_{3}-3}|,

holds with probability at least
1-d^{-\Omega(t)}
.

Proof.

The main idea is to use matrix Bernstein bound after matricizing the third-order tensor. Similar to the proof of Lemma
E.5
, we consider each node component individually and then sum up the errors and apply union bound.

We have shown the bound for
j_{3}=3,4
in Lemma
E.8
and Lemma
E.9
respectively.
To summarize, for any
0<\epsilon<1
we have if

|S|\geq\max_{i\in[k]}\left(1+\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}/|m_{j_{3},i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{(j_{3}-3)}|^{2}\right)\cdot\epsilon^{-2}\cdot k^{2}\poly(\log d,t)

with probability at least
1-d^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|R_{3}-\widehat{R}_{3}\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{j_{3},i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{j_{3}-3}|.

∎

E.5
Error Bound for the Magnitude and Sign of the Weight Vectors

The lemmata in this section together with Lemma
E.5
provide guarantees for Algorithm
4
. In particular, Lemma
E.12
shows with linear sample complexity in
d
, we can approximate the 1st-order moment to some precision. And Lemma
E.13
and Lemma
E.14
provide the error bounds of linear systems Eq. (
28
) under some perturbations.

E.5.1
Robustness for Solving Linear Systems

Lemma E.11

(Robustness of linear system)
.

Given two matrices
A,\widetilde{A}\in\R^{d\times k}
, and two vectors
b,\widetilde{b}\in\R^{d}
. Let
z^{*}=\argmin_{z\in\R^{k}}\|Az-b\|
and
\widehat{z}=\argmin_{z\in\R^{k}}\|(A+\widetilde{A})z-(b+\widetilde{b})\|
. If
\|\widetilde{A}\|\leq\frac{1}{4\kappa}\sigma_{k}(A)
and
\|\widetilde{b}\|\leq\frac{1}{4}\|b\|
,
then, we have

\displaystyle\|z^{*}-\widehat{z}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle(\sigma_{k}^{-4}(A)\sigma_{1}^{2}(A)+\sigma_{k}^{-2}(A))\|b\|\|\widetilde{A}\|+\sigma_{k}^{-2}(A)\sigma_{1}(A)\|\widetilde{b}\|.

Proof.

By definition of
z
and
\widehat{z}
, we can rewrite
z
and
\widehat{z}
,

\displaystyle z=

\displaystyle\penalty\ A^{\dagger}b=(A^{\top}A)^{-1}A^{\top}b

\displaystyle\widehat{z}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (A+\widetilde{A})^{\dagger}(b+\widetilde{b})=((A+\widetilde{A})^{\top}(A+\widetilde{A}))^{-1}(A+\widetilde{A})^{\top}(b+\widetilde{b}).

As
\|\widetilde{A}\|\leq\frac{1}{4\kappa}\sigma_{k}(A)
, we have
\|\widetilde{A}^{\top}A+A^{\top}\widetilde{A}\|\|(A^{\top}A)^{-1}\|\leq 1/4
. Together with
\|\widetilde{b}\|\leq\frac{1}{4}\|b\|
, we can ignore the high-order errors.
So we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{z}-z^{*}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(A^{\top}A)^{-1}(\widetilde{A}^{\top}b+A^{\top}\widetilde{b})+(A^{\top}A)^{-1}(A^{\top}\widetilde{A}+\widetilde{A}^{\top}A)(A^{\top}A)^{-1}A^{\top}b\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|(A^{\top}A)^{-1}\|(\|\widetilde{A}\|\|b\|+\|A\|\|\widetilde{b}\|)+\|(A^{\top}A)^{-2}\|\cdot\|A\|\|\widetilde{A}\|\|A\|\|b\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sigma_{k}^{-2}(A)(\|\widetilde{A}\|\|b\|+\sigma_{1}(A)\|\widetilde{b}\|)+\sigma_{k}^{-4}(A)\cdot\sigma_{1}^{2}(A)\|\widetilde{A}\|\|b\|.

∎

E.5.2
Error Bound for the First-order Moment

Lemma E.12

(Error bound for the first-order moment)
.

Let
Q_{1}
be defined as in Eq. (
25
) and
\widehat{Q}_{1}
be the empirical version of
Q_{1}
using dataset
S
, where each sample of
S
is i.i.d. sampled from distribution
{\cal D}
(defined in (
1
)). Assume the activation function satisfies Property
3.1
and Assumption
5.3
. Then for any
0<\epsilon<1
and
t\geq 1
, define
j_{1}=\min\{j\geq 1|M_{j}\neq 0\}
and
m_{0}=\min_{i\in[k]}(m_{j_{1},i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{j_{1}-1})^{2}
if

|S|\geq\sigma_{1}^{2p+2}d\poly(t,\log d)/(\epsilon^{2}m_{0})

we have with probability at least
1-d^{-\Omega(t)}
,

\displaystyle\|Q_{1}-\widehat{Q}_{1}\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{j_{1},i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{j_{1}-1}|.

Proof.

We consider the case when
l_{1}=3
, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q_{1}=M_{3}(I,\alpha,\alpha)=\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}^{*}m_{3,i}(\alpha^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*})^{3}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}.

And other cases are similar.

Since
y=\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}^{*}\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)
. We consider each component
i\in[k]
.

Define function
B_{i}(x):\R^{d}\rightarrow\R^{d}
such that

\displaystyle B_{i}(x)=[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot(x^{\otimes 3}-x\widetilde{\otimes}I)](I,\alpha,\alpha)=\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\cdot((x^{\top}\alpha)^{2}x-2(x^{\top}\alpha)\alpha-x).

Define
g(z)=\phi(z)-\phi(0)
, then
|g(z)|=|\int_{0}^{z}\phi^{\prime}(s)\mathrm{d}s|\leq L_{1}/(p+1)|z|^{p+1}
, which follows Property
3.1
.

(I) Bounding
\|B_{i}(x)\|
.

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)|\cdot\|((x^{\top}\alpha)^{2}x-2\alpha^{\top}x\alpha-x)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (|w_{i}^{*\top}x|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)(((x^{\top}\alpha)^{2}+1)\|x\|+2|\alpha^{\top}x|)

According to Fact
B.1
and Fact
B.2
, we have for any constant
t\geq 1
, with probability
1-1/(nd^{t})
,

\displaystyle\|B_{i}(x)\|\lesssim(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)\sqrt{d}\poly(\log d,t)

(II) Bounding
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|
.

Note that
\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]=m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}
. Therefore,
\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]\|=|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}|
.

(III) Bounding
\max(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\|B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}\|,\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\|B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)\|)
.

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[B_{i}(x)^{\top}B_{i}(x)\right]\right\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(x^{\top}\alpha)^{8}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\|x\|^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}d.

\displaystyle\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[B_{i}(x)B_{i}(x)^{\top}\right]\right\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\phi(w_{i}^{*\top}x)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(x^{\top}\alpha)^{8}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\max_{\|a\|=1}\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(x^{\top}a)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|)^{2}.

(IV) Bounding
\max_{\|a\|=\|b\|=1}(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)b)^{2}])
.

\displaystyle\max_{\|a\|=1}\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[(a^{\top}B_{i}(x)a)^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\left(\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}\left[\phi^{4}(w_{i}^{*\top}x)\right]\right)^{1/4}\lesssim\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|.

Define
L=\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|
.
Then we have for any
0<\epsilon<1
, if

|S|\gtrsim\frac{L^{2}d+|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}|^{2}+L|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}|\sqrt{d}\poly(\log d,t)\epsilon}{\epsilon^{2}|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}|^{2}}\cdot t\log d

with probability at least
1-1/d^{t}
,

\left\|\E_{x\sim\D_{d}}[B_{i}(x)]-\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_{x\sim S}^{n}B_{i}(x)\right\|\leq\epsilon|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}|.

Summing up all
k
components, we obtain if

|S|\geq\max_{i\in[k]}\left\{\frac{(\|w_{i}^{*}\|^{p+1}+|\phi(0)|+|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}|)^{2}}{|m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}|^{2}}\right\}\cdot\epsilon^{-2}d\poly(\log d,t)

with probability at least
1-1/d^{t}
,

\displaystyle\|M_{3}(I,\alpha,\alpha)-\widehat{M}_{3}(I,\alpha,\alpha)\|\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{k}|v_{i}^{*}m_{3,i}(\overline{w}_{i}^{*\top}\alpha)^{2}|.

Other cases (
j_{1}=1,2,4
) are similar, so we complete the proof.
∎

E.5.3
Linear System for the First-order Moment

The following lemma provides estimation error bound for the first linear system in Eq. (
28
).

Lemma E.13

(Solution of linear system for the first order moment)
.

Let
U\in\R^{d\times k}
be the orthogonal column span of
W^{*}
. Let
V\in\R^{d\times k}
denote an orthogonal matrix satisfying that
\|VV^{\top}-UU^{\top}\|\leq\widehat{\delta}_{2}\lesssim 1/(\kappa^{2}\sqrt{k})
. For each
i\in[k]
, let
\widehat{u}_{i}
denote the vector satisfying
\|\widehat{u}_{i}-V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\leq\widehat{\delta}_{3}\lesssim 1/(\kappa^{2}\sqrt{k})
. Let
Q_{1}
be defined as in Eq. (
25
) and
\widehat{Q}_{1}
be the empirical version of
Q_{1}
such that
\|Q_{1}-\widehat{Q}_{1}\|\leq\widehat{\delta}_{4}\|Q_{1}\|\leq\frac{1}{4}\|Q_{1}\|
.
Let
z^{*}\in\R^{k}
and
\widehat{z}\in\R^{k}
be defined as in Eq. (
27
) and Eq. (
28
).
Then

\displaystyle|\widehat{z}_{i}-z_{i}^{*}|\leq(\kappa^{4}k^{3/2}(\widehat{\delta}_{2}+\widehat{\delta}_{3})+\kappa^{2}k^{1/2}\widehat{\delta}_{4})\|z^{*}\|_{1}.

Proof.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
denote the matrix where the
i
-th column is
s_{i}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}
. Let
\widetilde{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
denote the matrix where the
i
-th column is
V\widehat{u}_{i}
. Let
b\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
denote the vector
Q_{1}
, let
\widetilde{b}
denote the vector
\widehat{Q}_{1}-Q_{1}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\|A\|\leq\sqrt{k}.

Using Fact
B.3
, we can lower bound
\sigma_{k}(A)
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(A)\geq 1/\kappa.

We can upper bound
\|\widetilde{A}\|
in the following way,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{A}\|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{k}\max_{i\in[k]}\{\|V\widehat{u}_{i}-s_{i}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{k}\max_{i\in[k]}\{\|V\widehat{u}_{i}-s_{i}VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}+s_{i}VV^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}-s_{i}UU^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{k}(\widehat{\delta}_{3}+\widehat{\delta}_{2}).

We can upper bound
\|b\|
and
\|\widetilde{b}\|
,

\displaystyle\|b\|=\|Q_{1}\|\leq k\sum_{i=1}^{k}|z_{i}^{*}|,\text{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\|\widetilde{b}\|\leq\widehat{\delta}_{4}\|Q_{1}\|.

To apply Lemma
E.11
, we need
\widehat{\delta}_{4}\leq 1/4
and
\widehat{\delta}_{2}\lesssim 1/(\sqrt{k}\kappa^{2})
,
\widehat{\delta}_{3}\lesssim 1/(\sqrt{k}\kappa^{2})
. So we have,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{z}_{i}-z_{i}^{*}\|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\kappa^{4}k^{3/2}(\widehat{\delta}_{2}+\widehat{\delta}_{3})+\kappa^{2}k^{1/2}\widehat{\delta}_{4})\|Q_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\kappa^{4}k^{3/2}(\widehat{\delta}_{2}+\widehat{\delta}_{3})+\kappa^{2}k^{1/2}\widehat{\delta}_{4})\sum_{i=1}^{k}|z_{i}^{*}|.

∎

E.5.4
Linear System for the Second-order Moment

The following lemma provides estimation error bound for the second linear system in Eq. (
28
).

Lemma E.14

(Solution of linear system for the second order moment)
.

Let
U\in\R^{d\times k}
be the orthogonal column span of
W^{*}
denote an orthogonal matrix satisfying that
\|VV^{\top}-UU^{\top}\|\leq\widehat{\delta}_{2}\lesssim 1/(\kappa\sqrt{k})
. For each
i\in[k]
, let
\widehat{u}_{i}
denote the vector satisfying
\|\widehat{u}_{i}-V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}\|\leq\widehat{\delta}_{3}\lesssim 1/(\sqrt{k}\kappa^{3})
.
Let
Q_{2}
be defined as in Eq. (
26
) and
\widehat{Q}_{2}
be the estimation of
Q_{2}
such that
\|Q_{2}-\widehat{Q}_{2}\|_{F}\leq\widehat{\delta}_{4}\|Q_{2}\|_{F}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|Q_{2}\|_{F}
. Let
r^{*}\in\R^{k}
and
\widehat{r}\in\R^{k}
be defined as in Eq. (
27
) and Eq. (
28
).
Then

\displaystyle|\widehat{r}_{i}-r_{i}^{*}|\leq(k^{3}\kappa^{8}\widehat{\delta}_{3}+\kappa^{2}k^{2}\widehat{\delta}_{4})\|r^{*}\|.

Proof.

For each
i\in[k]
, let
u_{i}=V^{\top}\overline{w}_{i}^{*}
. Let
A\in\R^{k^{2}\times k}
denote the matrix where the
i
-th column is
\text{vec}(u_{i}u_{i}^{\top})
. Let
\widetilde{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{2}\times k}
denote the matrix where the
i
-th column is
\text{vec}(u_{i}u_{i}^{\top}-\widehat{u}_{i}\widehat{u}_{i}^{\top})
. Let
b\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{2}}
denote the vector
\text{vex}(Q_{2})
, let
\widetilde{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{2}}
denote the vector
\text{vec}(Q_{2}-\widehat{Q}_{2})
.

Let
\circ
be the element-wise matrix product (a.k.a. Hadamard product),
\overline{W}=[\overline{w}_{1}^{*}\;\overline{w}_{2}^{*}\;\cdots\;\overline{w}_{k}^{*}]
and
U=[u_{1}\;u_{2}\;\cdots\;u_{k}]=V^{\top}\overline{W}
.
We can upper bound
\|A\|
and
\|\widetilde{A}\|
as follows,

\displaystyle\|A\|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max_{\|x\|=1}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}x_{i}\text{vec}(u_{i}u_{i}^{\top})\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max_{\|x\|=1}\|U\mathrm{diag}(x)U^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|U\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sigma^{2}_{1}(V^{\top}\overline{W}),

and

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{A}\|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{k}\max_{i\in[k]}\|\widetilde{A}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{k}\max_{i\in[k]}\|u_{i}u_{i}^{\top}-\widehat{u}_{i}\widehat{u}_{i}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{k}\max_{i\in[k]}2\|u_{i}-\widehat{u}_{i}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ 2\sqrt{k}\widehat{\delta}_{3}.

We can lower bound
\sigma_{k}(A)
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(A)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{\sigma_{k}(A^{\top}A)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{\sigma_{k}((U^{\top}U)\circ(U^{\top}U))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{\|x\|=1}\sqrt{x^{\top}((U^{\top}U)\circ(U^{\top}U))x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{\|x\|=1}\|(U^{\top}U)^{1/2}\mathrm{diag}(x)(U^{\top}U)^{1/2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sigma^{2}_{k}(V^{\top}\overline{W})

where fourth step follows Schur product theorem, the last step follows by the fact that
\|CB\|_{F}\geq\sigma_{\min}(C)\|B\|_{F}
and
\circ
is the element-wise multiplication of two matrices.

We can upper bound
\|b\|
and
\|\widetilde{b}\|
,

\displaystyle\|b\|\leq

\displaystyle\|Q_{2}\|_{F}\leq\|r^{*}\|,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{b}\|=

\displaystyle\|Q_{2}-\widehat{Q}_{2}\|_{F}\leq\widehat{\delta}_{4}\|r^{*}\|.

Since
\|VV^{\top}\overline{W}-\overline{W}\|\leq\sqrt{k}\widehat{\delta}_{2}
, we have for any
x\in\R^{k}
,

\displaystyle\|VV^{\top}\overline{W}x\|\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\overline{W}x\|-\|(VV^{\top}\overline{W}-\overline{W})x\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sigma_{k}(\overline{W})\|x\|-\widehat{\delta}_{2}\sqrt{k}\|x\|

Note that according to Fact
B.3
,
\sigma_{k}(\overline{W})\geq 1/\kappa
. Therefore, if
\widehat{\delta}_{2}\leq 1/(2\kappa\sqrt{k})
, we will have
\sigma_{k}(V^{\top}\overline{W})\geq 1/(2\kappa)
.
Similarly, we have
\sigma_{1}(V^{\top}\overline{W})\leq\|V\|\|\overline{W}\|\leq\sqrt{k}
. Then applying Lemma
E.11
and setting
\widehat{\delta}_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}\kappa^{3}}
, we complete the proof.
∎

Appendix F
